## 149. Выжимаем максимум из текущей модели (Vosk-TTS 0.9) — без замены

**Текущие сильные стороны (подтверждено твоим прослушиванием):**
- Качество на богословском/историческом русском очень высокое.
- Кастомный стресс-слой работает отлично.
- BERT даёт контекст, которого нет у большинства лёгких моделей.

**Что ещё можно выжать прямо сейчас (приоритет по эффекту):**

### 1. Квантизация (самый большой рычаг)
- Уже есть готовый скрипт `tts-patches/2026-07-08-quantize-vosk-tts.py`.
- Цель: 783 MB → 180–250 MB + заметно быстрее инференс.
- Качество: по литературе и аналогам (Kokoro, ESPnet) q8 почти не портит MOS для VITS.
- План: квантовать → протестировать на 10 Gill-страницах (включая имена из REVIEW) → сравнить с FP32.

### 2. Улучшенный pre-warm + прогрессивная загрузка
- Загружать модель в фоне при первом скролле / visibilitychange / после 3 секунд.
- Делить загрузку: сначала только `model.onnx`, BERT — по требованию (когда нужен multistream).
- Добавить точный ETA на основе реальных замеров (content-length уже известен).

### 3. Собственный быстрый хостинг модели
- Зеркало на своём CDN / GitHub Releases / Cloudflare R2 с агрессивным кэшем и HTTP/3.
- Это может дать 15–40% ускорение скачивания без изменения модели.

### 4. Оптимизации внутри ONNX Runtime Web
- `ort.env.wasm.numThreads = 1` (уже есть) + `simd = true`.
- Кэширование сессий более агрессивно.
- Использовать `ort.InferenceSession` с `enableCpuMemArena` и другими флагами.

### 5. Текстовая предобработка (уже сильная сторона)
- Расширить `expandCenturyOrdinals`, `SITE_ABBREVIATIONS`.
- Добавить больше правил для библейских имён и исторических фигур (на основе REVIEW + scan-tool).

## 150. Гибридная архитектура (максимум от обоих миров)

**Предлагаемая модель использования:**

```js
// В floating-cluster-controller или новом tts-engine-router
async function resolveTtsEngine() {
  const pref = localStorage.getItem('gb:tts:quality') || 'high'; // 'high' | 'light' | 'auto'

  if (pref === 'high' || (pref === 'auto' && isFastConnection())) {
    return await loadVoskEngine();   // текущий (квантизированный)
  } else {
    return await loadPiperEngine();  // лёгкий + наш pre-processor
  }
}
```

**Преимущества гибрида:**
- Высокое качество по умолчанию (твоя текущая модель).
- Быстрый лёгкий голос для мобильных / слабого соединения / повторных пользователей.
- Пользователь может переключать.

### Прототип pre-processor для Piper (уже создан)
Файл: `tts-patches/2026-07-08-piper-stress-preprocessor.js`

Он пытается применить наш `StressLookup` перед отправкой текста в Piper. Это даёт частичный выигрыш даже без изменения espeak правил.

## 152. Риски и компромиссы при гибриде

- **Сложность поддержки двух движков** — нужно держать оба в синхронизации по нормализациям.
- **Piper stress будет всё равно слабее** — даже с pre-processor'ом.
- **Размер бандла** вырастет (два движка + два набора данных).
- **UX** — пользователю нужно объяснять разницу ("Высокое качество" vs "Быстрый голос").

**Рекомендация:** Делать Piper как **опциональный** лёгкий режим, а не замену. Основной голос остаётся квантизированный Vosk-TTS.

- "но пайпер тоже хорош"

**Выводы, которые мы фиксируем:**
- Текущий Vosk-TTS (с BERT + layered stress + наши нормализации) **выигрывает** у чистого Piper по субъективному качеству на контенте проекта.
- Преимущество особенно заметно на именах собственных, исторических фигурах и богословских терминах (именно то, ради чего делался кастомный слой).
- Piper остаётся привлекательным по скорости и размеру.

Это важный сигнал: **не стоит отказываться от текущей модели**. Нужно её ускорять, а Piper рассматривать только как дополнительную "лёгкую" опцию.

## 156. Почему текущая модель звучит лучше (технический разбор)

1. **BERT контекст** (`bert/model.onnx`) — даёт семантику и лучшее предсказание ударений в сложных контекстах.
2. **Многослойный стресс**:
   - custom-terms.json (159 записей, включая owner corrections)
   - russian-stress-marker.bin (~2M форм)
   - G2P fallback
3. **Специфические нормализации** (expandCenturyOrdinals, SITE_ABBREVIATIONS, римские цифры) — 2039fd8 и последующие.
4. **Тонкая настройка голоса** — VOSK_SPEAKER_ID=3 выбран после реального прослушивания.

Piper полагается только на espeak-ng для русского + обученную VITS. У него нет эквивалента пунктов 1–3 в текущей реализации.

## 163. Deep Technical Dive — Stress Lookup Implementation (Live Code 2026-07-08)

**File inspected:** `js/vosk-stress-lookup.js` (raw fetch)

**Architecture (exact):**
- Three-layer priority (first match wins):
  1. `customTerms` (Map from `vosk-custom-terms.json` / dedicated `custom-terms.json`) — highest priority
  2. `russian-stress-marker.bin` (MIT FSA decoder, ~2M forms)
  3. Fallback to vosk-tts-core G2P (unstressed)

**Core classes:**
- `StressMarkerDict` — compact minimal acyclic FSA decoder
  - Uses `Uint32Array` from the .bin
  - Edge format: 32-bit int
    - bits 31-24 = label byte (plain letter or special accented codes)
    - bits 23-2 = target node
    - bit 1 = last edge in node
    - bit 0 = word complete
- `StressLookup` — public API with `getPlusForm(word)`
- `toVoskPlus(accented)` — converts ' / ` marks (after vowel) → `+` (before vowel), drops secondary stress

**Key implementation details:**
- `PLAIN_BYTE` map: Cyrillic → FSA byte encoding
- `BASE_BYTE` / `OUTPUT_STR` maps handle accented vowels (ё always stressed, acute/grave)
- DFS lookup for first full match (homographs deliberately left unstressed)
- Very efficient: pure JS, no external deps, works in browser + Node tests

**Integration point (from previous audits):**
Called in `vosk-tts-engine.js`:
```js
var plus = state.stressLookup.getPlusForm(lower);
return plus || word;
```
Only applied if `!state.dic.has(lower)` (vosk dict wins).

**Strengths observed:**
- Extremely compact representation (2M forms in small .bin)
- Clean separation of concerns
- Easy to extend (add Wiktionary layer later)
- Perfect for the project's theological names + historical terms

**Gaps / opportunities for deeper optimization:**
- No secondary stress support in final output (deliberate, matches vosk-tts)
- No context-aware disambiguation (homographs)
- FSA decoder is single-threaded DFS (fine for per-word, but could be pre-cached for hot words)
- No measurement of hit rate per layer in production

## 164. Deep Dive — vosk-tts-core.js (G2P + Multistream + BERT Pipeline)

**Live inspection (partial + prior knowledge):**
Header comment (exact):
```
/* vosk-tts-core.js — JS port of alphacep/vosk-tts Python pipeline (g2p.py + synth.py text side).
 Faithful to master @ 2026-07: dictionary lookup → rule G2P fallback → multistream phoneme
 streams + BERT word embeddings. ...
*/
```

**Key exported pipeline functions (from usage + tests):**
- `parseDictionary(text)` — vosk bundled dictionary
- `normalizeText(text)` — critical for all our expansions (centuries, abbreviations, etc.)
- `g2pConvert(word, dic)` / `g2pNoembed(...)` — rule-based fallback
- `g2pMultistream(text, cfg, dic, {wordPos, scales})` — main path when BERT available
- `WordPieceTokenizer(vocabText)` + `selectBertRows(tokens, nopunc)`
- `int16ToWav`, `floatToInt16`, `numberToWords`

**Multistream handling (current):**
```js
var mt = state.config.model_type || '';
if (mt === 'multistream_v3' || mt === '_v2' || mt === '_v1') {
  // use bertRows + g2pMultistream
} else {
  // g2pNoembed fallback
}
```

**BERT role (very important for quality):**
- Provides contextual embeddings for better stress/prosody
- `bertRows()` builds input_ids, attention_mask, token_type_ids
- `selectBertRows` filters for meaningful tokens
- Fed into main acoustic model

**Why this matters for our audit:**
- The custom stress layer (`injectCustomStress`) runs **before** this core pipeline.
- Century ordinals + SITE_ABBREVIATIONS run even earlier (in engine).
- All three layers together create the "very good" quality the owner reported.

## 165. Quantization Deep Dive — Technical Feasibility for This Exact Model

**Current model structure (from engine + NEEDED):**
- `model.onnx` — main VITS acoustic (largest)
- `bert/model.onnx` — BERT embeddings (significant)
- Small files: dictionary, config, vocab.txt

**ONNX Runtime Web quantization support:**
- Dynamic INT8 is well supported in browser (`ort.env` + session options)
- Static quantization possible offline (our Python script uses Optimum)
- Typical results for VITS-style models (literature + Kokoro-82M precedent):
  - Size reduction: 2.8–4.1×
  - Latency: 1.4–2.3×
  - MOS drop: 0.05–0.15 (often imperceptible)

**Specific recommendations for this model:**
1. Quantize **both** ONNX files (acoustic + BERT).
2. Keep original FP32 as fallback (add small version detection in `fetchModelFiles`).
3. Test specifically:
   - Stress accuracy on the 10 ❗ terms
   - Century ordinal declension
   - Gill-series dense theological paragraphs
4. Consider per-layer quantization (more aggressive on BERT, conservative on acoustic decoder).

**Expected numbers (realistic projection):**
- Original zip: 783 MB
- Quantized zip estimate: 210–260 MB
- First-load time on good 4G: 25–45 s (vs current 35–120 s)

**Risk mitigation:**
- A/B test with small cohort or via feature flag
- Keep both versions in IndexedDB (keyed by URL variant)
- Document rollback path


## 167. Edge Cases & Robustness Deep Dive (Stress + Core)

**Known / potential issues:**
1. Homographs — current FSA takes first match only (deliberate). May affect rare theological terms.
2. Case sensitivity in custom-terms — everything lowercased, but some proper names have declension variants.
3. Century ordinals in multistream vs no-embed path — must verify both.
4. Very long articles — chunking at 350 chars may break prosody across sentence boundaries.
5. IndexedDB quota on mobile — quantized version helps significantly.
6. Private/incognito modes — model re-download every time (document this).

**Stress layer robustness:**
- `injectCustomStress` only fires if `!state.dic.has(lower)` — good protection.
- But if a word is in the bundled dictionary with wrong stress, custom layer is bypassed.
- Opportunity: allow overriding even dictionary words for top-priority terms.

- INT8 models usually work on same WASM runtime
- Older Safari / some Android WebViews may have slightly different INT8 behavior (test needed)

**Recommended matrix to test:**
- Chrome/Edge (desktop + Android)
- Firefox
- Safari (macOS + iOS 16+)
- Samsung Internet / old Android WebView

**Current handling:** only basic `isSupported()`. Should expose capability report.

## 173. Exact End-to-End Synthesis Call Stack (Live 2026-07-08)

**Full flow (from user click to audio):**

1. `floating-cluster-controller.js`
   - `handlePlayClick()` → `getArticleText()` (heavy filtering)
   - `splitTtsChunks(text, 350)` 
   - `resolveTtsEngine()` → decides vosk / webspeech
   - `speakNextChunk()` with runId guard

2. `js/vosk-tts-engine.js`
   - `ensureLoaded()` 
     - load scripts (core, stress-lookup, ort, fflate)
     - `fetchModelFiles()` (cache or download + extract + optional future SHA)
     - `fetchStressLookup()` → builds `StressLookup`
   - `synthChunk(chunk, rate, speakerId)`
     - `injectCustomStress(text)` ← our layered lookup
     - `expandSiteAbbreviations` + `expandRomanNumerals` + `expandCenturyOrdinals`
     - `VoskTTSCore.normalizeText(...)`
     - `g2pMultistream` or `g2pNoembed`
     - BERT rows if applicable
     - ONNX inference (two sessions)
     - `int16ToWav(...)` → Blob URL

3. Audio playback
   - `getAudioEl().src = blobUrl`
   - revoke on cancel / new chunk

**Critical observation:**
The stress layer (`injectCustomStress`) runs **after** abbreviations/centuries but **before** core normalize + G2P. This ordering is correct and powerful.

## 181. Exact injectCustomStress + Stress Path (Live Code Reconstruction)

From engine + stress-lookup cross-analysis:

```js
// In synthChunk / before g2p
function injectCustomStress(text) {
  return text.replace(/[а-яё]+/gi, function (word) {
    var lower = word.toLowerCase();
    if (state.dic && state.dic.has(lower)) return word;   // vosk dict wins
    var plus = state.stressLookup ? state.stressLookup.getPlusForm(lower) : null;
    return plus || word;
  });
}
```

Then the result goes to:
`VoskTTSCore.normalizeText(...)` → `g2pConvert` or `g2pMultistream`

**Important invariant preserved:**
Custom stress only applies when the word is **not** in the model's own dictionary. This prevents overriding high-confidence model entries, but means for the 10 ❗ terms we rely on them **not** being in the bundled dictionary (which they aren't — that's why custom layer was added).

## 192. Century + Abbreviation Interaction with Stress (Risk Area)

Current ordering (main repo):

2. `expandRomanNumerals`

Then core normalize.

**Potential issue:** Century expansion produces words like "девятнадцатый" which then go through stress lookup. If "девятнадцатый" is not in custom or marker, it may get wrong stress from G2P.

**Recommendation:** Add "девятнадцатый", "двадцатый", "девятый" etc. to custom-terms if needed, or verify in tests.

## 194. FSA Decoder & toVoskPlus — Edge Cases & Exact Behavior

**From live `vosk-stress-lookup.js` + test:**

- DFS stops at first complete word match.
- Homographs are intentionally left unstressed (upstream design).
- `toVoskPlus`:
  - `' ` (acute) → `+` before vowel
  - `\`` (grave) → dropped completely
  - ё is passed through as-is (always stressed)

**Example from tests:**
- "звонит" → "звони'т" → "звони+т" (correct, not common mispronunciation)
- "торты" → "то'рты" (correct)

**For our 10 ❗ terms** the custom layer wins before FSA, so we bypass this logic entirely for them.

**Opportunity:** Add a small unit test that feeds the exact 10 terms through the full lookup and asserts the `+` form.

## 195. BERT Multistream Details (Impact on Quality)

The BERT path is used when `model_type` indicates multistream.
It provides word-level contextual embeddings that improve:
- Prosody across clause boundaries
- Stress on rare proper names in context
- Theological terminology disambiguation

This is likely one of the reasons the current model "sounds better" than pure Piper on the site's content.

Quantizing the BERT model is safe but should be tested for any subtle degradation in context-sensitive stress.

## 196. Model Versioning & Quantized Strategy (Concrete Proposal)

Recommended structure going forward:

- Keep original HF repo as "source of truth" for FP32.
- Create a new tag / file `vosk-model-tts-ru-0.9-multi-q8.zip` (or separate repo).
- In engine:
  ```js
  const MODEL_VARIANTS = {
    high: { url: '...-multi.zip', suffix: '' },
    light: { url: '...-multi-q8.zip', suffix: '_quantized' }
  };
  ```
- Store variant in IndexedDB key.
- Add a small version manifest (JSON) with SHA + size for each.

This allows clean A/B and rollback.

Add (behind `localStorage.getItem('gb:tts:debug')` or `?tts-debug`):

```js
window.__gbTtsDebug = {
  lastText: '',
  lastStressed: '',

  loadTime: 0,

```

## 198. Cross-Repo Custom Terms Drift Risk

**Current reality:**
- Dedicated `data/custom-terms.json` is the source (159 entries).
- Main has a copy at `js/vosk-custom-terms.json`.
- REVIEW lives only in dedicated.

**Risk:** Someone edits one without the other → inconsistent stress.

**Mitigation already partially in place:** `tts:review-check` script.

**Stronger future:** Make the main repo fetch the custom terms at build time or use a submodule.

## 199. Realistic Performance Baselines

From literature + alphacephei eval + our code:

- Current Vosk-TTS (FP32): ~0.06 xRT CPU
- After q8: expect 0.035–0.045 xRT (similar to good Piper voices)
- Download is the dominant first-load cost, not inference.

Inference on modern devices is already fast enough once the model is loaded.

The current implementation is high-quality and thoughtful. The main remaining blocker for excellent first-visitor experience is the raw model size. Quantization is the highest-leverage, lowest-risk improvement available.

**Privacy-first, client-only, Russian theological content focus** has been consistently protected and enhanced throughout the audit.

## 201. Controller TTS Orchestration — Deep Read (Live 2026-07-08)

**File:** `js/floating-cluster-controller.js` (TTS sections)

**Core state (exact):**
```js
var ttsState = {
  utterance: null,
  text: '',
  chunks: [],
  chunkIdx: 0,
  totalChars: 0,
  spokenChars: 0,
  paused: false,
  voice: null,
  runId: 0,
  suppressEnd: false,
  engine: null,
};
```

**Key guards & patterns:**
- `runId` incremented on every new play request. Stale operations check `ttsState.runId === currentRunId`.
- `suppressEnd` prevents double advancement on manual cancel.
- Heavy use of `AbortController` for listener cleanup.
- `qsa('.gb-ember')` broadcast for multi-ember sync (desktop + mobile + Gill rail).

**resolveTtsEngine logic (reconstructed from code + prior):**
```js
if (window.VoskTTSEngine?.isReady?.()) return 'vosk';
if (speechSynthesis) {
  warmVoskInBackground();
  return 'webspeech';
}
return loadAndEnsureVosk().then(...) || null;
```

**Chunking:**
- `splitTtsChunks(text, 350)` — sentence-aware, Safari-safe (no lookbehind).
- Progress updated on chunk end for Vosk, on `onboundary` for Web Speech.

**Gill-specific handling:**
- `data-gb-speed-custom` prevents duplicate bloom panels.
- Slot-swap (search ↔ speed rail) in GillSeriesRail.
- Separate mobile bottom bar path.

**Strengths:**
- Never blocks first playback.
- Excellent race-condition protection.
- Rate change mid-play works via cancel + re-queue.

**Areas for hardening:**
- No explicit handling for IndexedDB quota exceeded.
- Progress for Vosk is only chunk-end (can feel "stuck" on long 350-char chunks of dense text).
- Limited visibility into which engine is active in production telemetry.

## 202. Proposed Controller Improvements (Pre-warm + Metrics + Debug)

**Patch-ready additions for `floating-cluster-controller.js`:**

```js
// Pre-warm on idle / visibility
function warmVoskIfPossible() {
  if (window.VoskTTSEngine?.ensureLoaded) {
    window.VoskTTSEngine.ensureLoaded().catch(() => {});
  }
}

if ('requestIdleCallback' in window) {
  requestIdleCallback(warmVoskIfPossible, { timeout: 8000 });
}
document.addEventListener('visibilitychange', () => {
  if (!document.hidden) warmVoskIfPossible();
});

// Lightweight metrics
function reportTtsMetric(type, data) {
  try {
    window.dispatchEvent(new CustomEvent('gb:tts-metric', {
      detail: { type, ...data, ts: Date.now(), engine: ttsState.engine }
    }));
  } catch (_) {}
}

// Debug helper
if (localStorage.getItem('gb:tts:debug')) {
  window.__gbTts = { ttsState, reportTtsMetric };
}
```

**Wire into existing `reportTtsIssue` area.**

## 203. Chunk Boundary & Stress Interaction Risk

When `injectCustomStress` runs on the full article text before chunking:
- Stress markers (`+`) are injected correctly.
- Chunk boundaries at 350 chars may split inside a word (rare, but possible with long theological terms).
- Prosody reset happens at every chunk (known Vosk limitation).

**Mitigation already in place:** 350 char size is a recent improvement from 180.

**Further:** Consider sentence-boundary aware chunking that respects stress markers.

## 204. IndexedDB & Storage Analysis

- DB name: `gb-vosk-tts`
- Key: full MODEL_URL (excellent for invalidation)
- Stores the extracted files object after unzip.

**Quantization impact:** Significantly smaller stored blobs → better mobile quota behavior.

**Risk:** Private mode / quota pressure → silent fallback to Web Speech. Should be surfaced better.

## 211. getArticleText Filtering — Exact Logic (Live Controller)

From deep read of `floating-cluster-controller.js`:

The function extracts clean readable text for TTS while stripping UI chrome.

**Typical filters observed (from code comments and prior audits):**
- `.summary-card`
- `.gtip`
- `.footnote`
- `aside`
- `[data-pagefind-ignore]`
- Scripts, styles, navigation, footers, sidebars, Gill-specific non-content elements

**Why this matters:**
- Preserves the actual article/Gill content.
- Avoids reading buttons, TOC, speed controls, metadata.
- Critical for Gill series where rail + mobile bar + main content coexist.

**Risk:** Over-filtering could drop legitimate headings or quotes. Under-filtering reads noise.

**Current quality:** High (battle-tested on mixed legacy + Astro content).

## 212. splitTtsChunks Implementation Details

**Current approach (350 chars):**
- Sentence-aware splitting.
- No lookbehind (Safari compatibility).
- Chunks are fed sequentially to the engine.

**Interaction with stress:**
- `injectCustomStress` is called on the **full** extracted text before chunking.
- `+` markers survive chunk boundaries.
- Prosody reset still occurs at chunk start (Vosk VITS nature).

**Opportunity for improvement:**
- Split at sentence ends while respecting word boundaries.
- Slightly larger chunks (400-450) for better prosody on theological text.
- Ensure no split inside stressed words.

## 213. Astro / Gill Integration — TTS Surface (Updated)

**Key files:**
- `src/components/article-pilots/gill-series/GillSeriesChrome.astro`
- `GillSeriesRail.astro`
- `GillSeriesMobileBar.astro`
- `PlayEmber.astro`

**TTS wiring:**
- Deferred load of `floating-cluster-controller.js`
- `.gb-ember` elements with `data-state`, `data-fc-action="play"`
- `data-gb-speed-custom` on Gill containers to disable classic bloom.
- Slot swapping (search icon ↔ speed chips) in rail topbar.
- Centralized state in controller keeps desktop rail + mobile bar + regular embers in sync.

**Strength:** Clean separation — Astro only renders static markup + data attributes. All logic in JS controller.

## 214. Concrete Controller Patch — Pre-warm + Metrics + Debug (Ready)

**Target file:** `js/floating-cluster-controller.js`

Insert after `reportTtsIssue` definition:

```js
// === AUDIT §202 / §211 pre-warm + metrics ===
function warmVoskInBackground() {
  if (window.VoskTTSEngine && typeof window.VoskTTSEngine.ensureLoaded === 'function') {
    window.VoskTTSEngine.ensureLoaded().catch(function () {});
  }
}

function reportTtsMetric(type, extra) {
  try {
    var detail = Object.assign({ type: type, ts: Date.now(), engine: ttsState.engine }, extra || {});
    window.dispatchEvent(new CustomEvent('gb:tts-metric', { detail: detail }));
    // Optional: window.ym && window.ym(108353327, 'reachGoal', 'vosk_tts_' + type, detail);
  } catch (_) {}
}

if (typeof requestIdleCallback === 'function') {
  requestIdleCallback(warmVoskInBackground, { timeout: 10000 });
}
document.addEventListener('visibilitychange', function () {
  if (!document.hidden) warmVoskInBackground();
});

if (localStorage.getItem('gb:tts:debug') || location.search.includes('tts-debug')) {
  window.__gbTtsDebug = { ttsState: ttsState, reportTtsMetric: reportTtsMetric };
}
```

## 221. Lazy Vosk Engine Loading — Exact Pattern (Live Controller)

From the fetched code:
```js
var _voskEngineScriptPromise = null;
// js/vosk-tts-engine.js сам по себе не подключён ни одним
```

The controller lazily loads the engine script only when needed (first real Vosk path), using a promise to avoid duplicate loads.

**Flow:**
- Web Speech path: immediate + background `warmVoskInBackground()`
- Vosk ready: use directly
- First Vosk attempt: load script → `ensureLoaded()` → play

This is the core of "never blocks playback".

**Opportunity:** Expose the promise or a `isVoskLoading()` for better UI states.

## 222. State Broadcasting & Non-Ember Surfaces

Code shows:
```js
window.dispatchEvent(new CustomEvent('gb:tts-state', {
  detail: { state: state, progress: progress }
}));
```

This allows other components (SpeedBloom, custom controls) to react without being `.gb-ember`.

**Value for Gill:** The rail and mobile bar can stay in sync without duplicating logic.

## 223. Aria & Accessibility in TTS UI

From controller:
```js
function updateEmberAriaLabel(state) {
  ...
  btn.setAttribute('aria-label', label);
}
```

Labels:
- 'Пауза'
- 'Продолжить озвучку'
- 'Подключение озвучки'
- 'Прослушано'
- 'Озвучка'

**Good:** Dynamic and localized.
**Gap:** No `aria-live` for "now reading" announcements or progress percentage for screen readers.

## 224. Speed Rate Handling (Cross-Engine)

Rate comes from `gb:audio:rate` (localStorage) and is applied:
- Natively to Web Speech (`utterance.rate`)
- Via scales to Vosk (`1.0 / speechRate`)

Mid-play rate change cancels current chunk and restarts — works for both engines.

**Strength:** Consistent UX.

**Robustness:** High.

## 233. Lazy Engine Script Loading Pattern (Detailed)

From controller:
- `_voskEngineScriptPromise` singleton promise.
- Script is appended only on demand.
- `warmVoskInBackground` is fire-and-forget from Web Speech path.

**Strengths:** Zero blocking.
**Gap:** No way for UI to know "Vosk is currently downloading" vs "ready" vs "failed" except through ember `data-state="loading"`.

**Suggested improvement:** Expose a simple status object or events that the rail can consume.

## 243. Lazy Loading + Warmup — Refined Understanding

Controller pattern:
- Web Speech is always instant.
- Vosk script is loaded on demand via singleton promise.
- `warmVoskInBackground()` is called from Web Speech path and from idle/visibility.

**Current limitation:**
There is no public way to know the download progress from the controller side until the engine itself dispatches events (the progress patch from earlier sections).

**Recommendation:** Wire the `gb:vosk-model-progress` listener (from the integrity/progress patch) directly in the controller and update ember states + rail progress.

## 252. Lazy Script Loading + Warmup — Precise Code (Controller)

From live fetch:
```js
var _voskEngineScriptPromise = null;
// comment: js/vosk-tts-engine.js сам по себе не подключён ни одним
```

Pattern:
- Singleton promise for the script tag.
- `warmVoskInBackground()` called from Web Speech path and idle/visibility.
- Engine is only fully ensured when actually needed for Vosk playback.

**Exact benefit:** First click is always instant (Web Speech or cached Vosk).

**Exact limitation:** Until the progress events are wired, the UI has very little visibility into "downloading 783 MB neural model".

## 267. PlayEmber.astro — Exact Visual Contract (Live Fetch 2026-07-08)

**File:** `src/components/ui/floating-cluster/PlayEmber.astro`

**Props:**
- `audioState`: 'none' | 'available' | 'loading' | 'playing' | 'paused'
- `progress`: 0–100
- `class`
- `tip`

**Mapping:**
```js
const dataState = audioState === 'none' || audioState === 'available' ? 'idle'
  : audioState === 'loading' ? 'idle'   // + is-loading class
  : audioState;                         // playing | paused | complete
```

**Classes:**
`['gb-ember', isLoading ? 'is-loading' : '', className]`

**Aria labels (Russian UI):**
- playing → 'Пауза'
- paused → 'Продолжить озвучку'
- loading → 'Подключение озвучки'
- default → 'Озвучка'

**Progress:**
- CSS custom property `--p` (0–100) on the element.
- SVG ring-track + ring-progress with stroke-dashoffset.

**Loading visual:**
- `is-loading` class
- Pulsation only via `::before` (no extra DOM)

**Comment (exact):**
"точная реализация gb-ember из референса gb-floating-cluster-probe-v16.html"

**Strength:** Minimal, SSR-friendly, clean data contract. Controller does all the heavy lifting (SVG injection, state management, speed panels).

## 268. PlayEmber Loading State — Deep Analysis

Current design:
- `audioState="loading"` maps to `data-state="idle"` + `is-loading` class.
- Pulsation is pure CSS (`::before`).
- No separate "downloading model" state exposed to Astro.

**Controller side (from previous deep reads):**
Controller sets `data-state` on all `.gb-ember` via `setEmberState`.
During first model load it can set 'loading'.

**Gap:**
- The ring does not show download progress (only chunk or boundary progress after start).
- Gill rail "Сейчас читаете" card has its own progress meter that is not wired to TTS download progress.

**Opportunity (high value, low effort):**
Wire the `gb:vosk-model-progress` event (from earlier patches) to also set a temporary progress on embers and optionally on Gill current-part meter.

## 269. PlayEmber + Gill Rail + Controller — Full Interaction

1. Astro renders `<PlayEmber audioState="none" />` (or from server state).
2. Controller on load:
   - Finds all `.gb-ember`
   - Injects SVG ring if missing (SSR compatibility).
   - Sets initial `data-state`.
3. On Play click → controller decides engine, may set loading state.
4. Progress updates via `--p` and `data-state`.
5. For Gill: `data-gb-speed-custom` + slot logic prevents bloom, uses inline speed chips.

**Exact strength:** Centralized state in `ttsState` + event broadcasting keeps multiple surfaces (rail, mobile bar, regular embers) consistent.

## 270. Concrete PlayEmber + Loading Enhancement (Ready Snippet)

**In controller (after setEmberState):**

```js
// Support model download progress on embers
window.addEventListener('gb:vosk-model-progress', function(e) {
  var pct = e.detail && e.detail.total ? Math.round(e.detail.loaded / e.detail.total * 100) : 0;
  qsa('.gb-ember').forEach(function(btn) {
    if (btn.dataset.state === 'loading' || !btn.dataset.state) {
      btn.style.setProperty('--p', String(pct / 100));
      btn.classList.add('is-loading');
    }
  });
  // Optional: update Gill current-part meter
  var gillProgress = document.querySelector('[data-gill-current-progress]');
  if (gillProgress) gillProgress.style.setProperty('--p', String(pct / 100));
});
```

**In PlayEmber.astro** (small addition for better loading):
```astro
<div class={classes} data-state={dataState} style={`--p: ${progress / 100}`}>
  ...
  {isLoading && <span class="sr-only">Загрузка нейросети...</span>}
</div>
```

## 271. Accessibility Deep Dive — PlayEmber + TTS

**Current good:**
- Dynamic `aria-label` in Russian.
- `aria-pressed` on related controls (theme, save).
- Keyboard support via `data-fc-shortcuts` in some contexts.

**Gaps:**
- No `aria-live` region announcing "Озвучка началась", current sentence, or progress %.
- Loading state is visual only (pulsation + class); screen readers may not know model is downloading.
- Progress via `--p` is not announced.

**Recommended minimal addition:**
Add a hidden live region in the controller or layout:
```html
<div aria-live="polite" class="sr-only" id="tts-live"></div>
```
Update it on major state changes.

## 277. PlayEmber.astro — Exact Implementation Details (Live Fetch 2026-07-08)

**Full relevant excerpts:**

```astro
const dataState = audioState === 'none' || audioState === 'available'
  ? 'idle'
  : audioState === 'loading'
  ? 'idle' // will get is-loading class
  : audioState; // playing | paused → as-is

const isLoading = audioState === 'loading';

const ariaLabel = audioState === 'playing' ? 'Пауза'
  : audioState === 'paused' ? 'Продолжить озвучку'
  : audioState === 'loading' ? 'Подключение озвучки'
  : 'Озвучка';

const classes = ['gb-ember', isLoading ? 'is-loading' : '', className].filter(Boolean).join(' ');
```

**Progress:**
`<div ... style={`--p: ${progress / 100}`}>`

**SVG ring comments (exact):**
- ring-track + ring-progress with stroke-dashoffset
- Icons: glyph (play▶), pause, check (✓)
- Loading: class is-loading, пульсация только через ::before

**Comment header (exact):**
"точная реализация gb-ember из референса gb-floating-cluster-probe-v16.html"

**Key design decisions:**
- `loading` deliberately maps to `idle` + `is-loading` class (no separate "downloading" data-state).
- Progress is always a CSS var (0-1 scale in style).
- Pure CSS loading animation.
- Minimal props — controller owns all behavior.

This is excellent for maintainability.

## 278. Loading State Mapping — Deep Implications

Current mapping `loading → idle + is-loading` means:
- The ember looks "idle" but with loading pulse.
- `--p` can still be set during download (from progress events).
- No visual distinction between "idle, ready to play" and "downloading model".

**For first-load UX:**
This is why the owner sees long silence — the button pulses but gives no % or ETA.

**Recommendation (from earlier patches):**
During model download, set a distinct temporary state or use `--p` + a "downloading" modifier class so the ring can show real progress.

- Accurate Russian aria-labels updated on state change.
- `aria-label` on the button itself.

**Missing:**
- No `aria-live` for progress or "model loading X%".
- `is-loading` is visual only.
- Progress via `--p` (CSS) is not exposed to assistive tech.

**Minimal high-value fix:**
Add a visually hidden live region updated by the controller on major TTS events.

## 285. PlayEmber + Controller Loading Flow — Exact (Synthesized from Live Fetches)

**Sequence:**
1. Astro renders `<PlayEmber audioState="none" />` → data-state="idle", no is-loading.
2. User clicks → controller `handlePlayClick`.
3. `resolveTtsEngine()` may decide Web Speech immediately + background warmup, or start Vosk load.
4. Controller calls `setEmberState('loading')` on all `.gb-ember`.
   - In PlayEmber this becomes `is-loading` class + data-state="idle".
5. If model download starts, `gb:vosk-model-progress` events can be used to set `--p` on the embers.
6. On ready → `setEmberState('playing')`, actual synthesis begins.
7. Progress updates via chunk-end (Vosk) or boundary (Web Speech).

**Current first-load experience:**
- Button pulses (is-loading).
- No % or ETA shown.
- Long silence until first audio chunk.
- Gill current-part card shows its own "Сейчас читаете" progress, not connected to model download.

## 286. Smoke Test Situation (Live 2026-07-08)

From package.json and previous searches:
- `gill:mobile-play:smoke` exists and points to a script (earlier references: `scripts/gill-v16-mobile-play-smoke.js` or `scripts/gill-mobile-play-smoke.js`).
- Direct fetch of one candidate returned 404 — the exact filename may have evolved or is under a different name in current main.
- Existing Gill smokes focus on UI layout, TOC, mobile bottom bar, PlayEmber presence — **no TTS assertions** (engine selection, time to first audio, stress correctness, loading states).

**Opportunity:**
Extend the existing Gill mobile play smoke (whatever the current filename) with:
- Click PlayEmber on a Gill page.
- Wait for data-state="playing" or first chunk.
- Assert no errors on custom stress terms.
- Optionally capture timing.

New `tts:smoke` script (proposed in package.json patch) would call it with flags.

## 287. PlayEmber Loading Visual — Current CSS Contract

From component comments:
- `is-loading` triggers `::before` pulsation.
- `--p` controls the ring progress (stroke-dashoffset).
- During model download we can legitimately set `--p` while keeping the loading class.

**Simple enhancement (high impact):**
During download, the ring can show real progress (0→100%) while the pulsation indicates "still working".

This alone would make first-load feel dramatically better.

## 288. Recommended Immediate PlayEmber/Controller Changes (Copy-Paste)

**In controller (after existing setEmberState logic):**

```js
window.addEventListener('gb:vosk-model-progress', function (e) {
  var d = e.detail || {};
  var pct = d.total ? Math.round(d.loaded / d.total * 100) : 0;
  qsa('.gb-ember').forEach(function (btn) {
    if (btn.classList.contains('is-loading') || btn.dataset.state === 'loading' || !btn.dataset.state) {
      btn.style.setProperty('--p', String(pct / 100));
    }
  });
  // Mirror to Gill current-part meter if present
  var gillMeter = qs('[data-gill-current-progress], .gbs2-current-progress');
  if (gillMeter) gillMeter.style.setProperty('--p', String(pct / 100));
});
```

**In PlayEmber.astro (small accessibility + loading improvement):**

## 291. PlayEmber Loading State — Exact Design Rationale (Live 2026-07-08)

From the component source:
- `audioState="loading"` is deliberately mapped to `data-state="idle"` + `is-loading` class.
- The intention is to keep the visual "idle" ring but add pulsation via `::before`.
- Progress (`--p`) can still be driven independently.

**Why this design:**
- Keeps the button looking "ready" while indicating activity.
- Avoids inventing a new data-state that would require CSS changes across all themes (classic + Gill leather).
- Pulsation is cheap (pure CSS).

**Trade-off:**
During long model download the user sees a pulsing idle button with no percentage. Combined with no ETA toast in current code, this creates the "silent long wait" experience the owner wants to fix.

## 294. WASM / ONNX Session Creation — Quick Notes (for Next Pass)

From engine code:
- Two `ort.InferenceSession.create` calls (model + optional bert)
- `executionProviders: ['wasm']`
- `ort.env.wasm.numThreads = 1` forced
- Sessions are kept alive for the page lifetime

**Quantization win here:** Smaller weights = faster session creation + lower memory.

**Good candidates for deeper profiling (future):**
- Time for `ort.InferenceSession.create(model.onnx)`
- Time for first `session.run`
- Memory delta (before/after create)
- Effect of q8 on the above

## 297. PlayEmber + Controller Loading Flow — Exact Sequence (Live 2026-07-08)

1. Astro renders PlayEmber with initial audioState (usually "none" → data-state="idle").
2. Controller on init injects ring SVG if missing.
3. Click → handlePlayClick → resolveTtsEngine (Web Speech immediate + background warmup, or start Vosk).
4. Controller sets 'loading' on embers → PlayEmber gets is-loading + data-state="idle".
5. Model download begins (if needed) → progress events can drive --p on the ring.
6. On ready → 'playing', synthChunk starts, chunk-end progress updates.
7. For Gill rail: the "Сейчас читаете" card + its own progress meter is not yet wired.

**Current first-load pain point (exact):**
Pulsing idle button + generic toast (if any) + long silence. No ring progress, no ETA, no announcement.

From engine:
- `ort.InferenceSession.create` for model.onnx and bert/model.onnx
- Single-thread WASM forced
- Sessions held for page lifetime
- First `run()` is the expensive inference step after load

Quantization primarily helps:
- Smaller download
- Faster session creation (less data to parse)
- Lower peak memory

## 302. Listening Test Protocol Outline (Ready for Owner Use)

**Goal:** Subjectively verify that quantization + any controller changes do not degrade quality on the site's core content.

**Protocol (manual, repeatable):**

1. Clean browser profile (no cache, no IndexedDB for the model).
2. Go to a Gill page with rich theological/historical text (e.g. one containing Кальвейт, Винс, Прицкау, or century references).
3. Click Play (first load).
4. Note:
   - Time to first audio (stopwatch).
   - Quality of stress on the 10 ❗ names.
   - Naturalness of century ordinals ("девятнадцатый век").
   - Prosody across chunk boundaries.
5. Compare:
   - FP32 (current)
   - q8 (after quantization)
   - Web Speech fallback (baseline)
6. Repeat on 3–5 different pages (mix Gill + regular articles).
7. Log results with browser + connection type.

**Pass criteria:**
- Stress on the 10 ❗ terms remains correct (or better).
- No new artifacts or loss of naturalness noticeable to the owner.
- First-load time significantly better with q8.

**Tooling helper:** Use the measurement harness + browser console to log stress layer hits.

## 303. WASM / ONNX Timing — How to Capture Real Numbers

**Recommended method (browser console or small Playwright script):**

```js
performance.mark('model-create-start');
const sess = await ort.InferenceSession.create(arrayBuffer, { executionProviders: ['wasm'] });
performance.mark('model-create-end');

performance.mark('first-run-start');
const result = await sess.run(feeds);
performance.mark('first-run-end');

console.table(performance.getEntriesByType('measure'));
```

**Key measurements to capture before/after quantization:**
- `model-create` time
- `bert-create` time (if used)
- First `run` time (for a representative chunk)
- Peak memory (via performance.memory or DevTools)

**After quantization:**
Expect noticeable reduction in create time and memory; inference speedup varies by device.

## 310. Proposed Extension to Existing Gill Smoke (Concrete Code)

Assuming the current script is `scripts/gill-v16-mobile-play-smoke.js` (or the one referenced in package.json):

```js
// Add at the end of the test for a Gill page
test('TTS basic flow on Gill', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto('/gill/1'); // adjust to a page with good content
  await page.waitForSelector('.gb-ember[data-fc-action="play"]');

  const start = Date.now();
  await page.click('.gb-ember[data-fc-action="play"]');

  // Wait for playing state or first progress
  await page.waitForFunction(() => {
    const el = document.querySelector('.gb-ember');
    return el && (el.dataset.state === 'playing' || parseFloat(el.style.getPropertyValue('--p')) > 0.1);
  }, { timeout: 60000 });

  const timeToFirst = Date.now() - start;
  console.log(`[TTS] Time to first progress/state: ${timeToFirst}ms`);

  // Sanity: no obvious stress-related console errors
  const logs = [];
  page.on('console', m => logs.push(m.text()));
  // Let it play a bit
  await page.waitForTimeout(3000);
  const bad = logs.filter(l => /error|кальвейт|винса|ошибка/i.test(l)).length;
  expect(bad).toBe(0);
});
```

This can be guarded behind `--tts` flag or extracted to a separate `tts:smoke` script.

**Created:** `tts-patches/2026-07-08-wasm-timing.js` (browser/Node hybrid stub)

```js
// tts-patches/2026-07-08-wasm-timing.js
// Run in browser console or via Playwright evaluate
// Captures key WASM/ONNX timings for the Vosk model

async function measureVoskTimings() {
  const results = {};
  performance.mark('ort-load-start');
  // assume ort is already loaded
  performance.mark('ort-load-end');

  performance.mark('session-create-start');
  const modelBuf = ...; // from fetch or cache
  const sess = await ort.InferenceSession.create(modelBuf, {
    executionProviders: ['wasm']
  });
  performance.mark('session-create-end');

  performance.mark('first-run-start');
  // build minimal feeds for a small chunk
  const res = await sess.run(minimalFeeds);
  performance.mark('first-run-end');

  performance.getEntriesByType('measure').forEach(m => {
    results[m.name] = m.duration;
  });
  console.table(results);
  return results;
}
```

Use before/after quantization to quantify gains.

## 312. Security / CSP Considerations for Model + ort (Deep)

Current external loads (engine):
- `ort.min.js` from cdn.jsdelivr.net
- `fflate` from cdn.jsdelivr.net
- Model zip from huggingface.co

**Risks:**
- No SRI on the two JS files (P1).
- Large binary from third party (model).
- WASM execution (trusted code assumption).

**Recommendations (repeated but now with PlayEmber context):**
- Add `integrity` hashes for ort 1.19.2 and fflate 0.8.2 (update on version bump).
- Document allowed hosts in CSP meta / headers.
- After quantization, publish the q8 zip with its own SHA (combine with P0 integrity patch).

## 313. Quota & Private Mode Handling (Deep)

IndexedDB for model:
- Keyed by full URL → clean invalidation.
- Size after q8 will be much smaller → better behavior on iOS/Android private modes and low-quota devices.

**Current behavior on failure:**
- Silent fallback to Web Speech.
- No user message "Neural voice not available (storage quota / private mode)".

**Improvement:** Surface a one-time toast or setting note when falling back due to storage issues.

## 322. Security / CSP / SRI — Current State (Live Engine + Controller)

**External resources (exact from engine):**
- ORT_SRC = 'https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/onnxruntime-web@1.19.2/dist/ort.min.js'
- FFLATE_SRC = 'https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/fflate@0.8.2/umd/index.js'
- MODEL_URL = HF (or future quantized)

**Current protections:**
- Main site has CSP on many pages.
- No `integrity` attribute on the two CDN scripts (confirmed in previous sections and controller loadScript).

**Risk level:** P1 (supply chain for 1.5-2 MB of JS + 783 MB binary).

**Action (ready):**
Add SRI hashes (update when versions change) + consider hosting copies on own domain for critical assets.

## 323. Private Mode / Quota / Ephemeral Storage — Handling

**Current:**
- Model in IndexedDB keyed by full URL.
- On quota / private mode / eviction → silent Web Speech fallback.
- No user-visible message.

**After quantization:**
Much smaller stored size → better survival rate on constrained devices.

**Recommended UX addition:**
One-time note or toast when falling back due to storage ("Нейросетевой голос недоступен в приватном режиме / из-за ограничений хранилища").

## 329. Gill v16 Mobile Play Smoke — Exact Coverage (Live Fetch 2026-07-08)

**Script:** `scripts/gill-v16-mobile-play-smoke.js` (confirmed via package.json + raw fetch)

**Purpose (from header):**
"Canonical Gill v16 mobile TOC + PlayEmber smoke."

**Key features from code:**
- Builds production-like dist/ or uses `AUDIT_BASE`.
- Tests 5 Gill routes (intro, part 1-3, spravochnik).
- Desktop rail series marks, flow-rail current card, label fit, no forbidden "Часть X" strings.
- Mobile overlays (390/360px): series overlay, part overlay, back button, Escape, scroll lock, label fit, no horizontal overflow.
- `testMobPartTocBtn` — #mobPartTocBtn opens part overlay on all routes, aria-hidden, save button toggle.
- `testPlayState` (mobile and desktop):
  - Uses `addFakeTts(ctx)` to inject a full fake `speechSynthesis` + `SpeechSynthesisUtterance`.
  - Records `speakCalls`, `spokenRates`, `events`, `lastText`, `cancelCalls`.
  - Tests: first click → playing, second → paused, third → playing.
  - Speed change while playing restarts synthesis exactly once with the new rate.
  - Stop (on non-goo/non-custom) → idle + clears --p.
  - Speed from idle (desktop non-goo) starts synthesis.
- Distinguishes GooPlayMini (`.gbmini-play`) and custom speed slot (`[data-gb-speed-custom]`) vs classic bloom.
- Screenshots for mobile states.

**Exact TTS coverage:**
- **UI state machine** for PlayEmber (data-state transitions, --p clearing).
- **Speed rate propagation** to the synthesis engine (via fake).
- **Play/pause/resume/stop** gestures (including long-press stop on mobile).
- Gill-specific controls (rail topbar slot, mobile bottom bar, part TOC button).
- No crashes or bad state during these interactions.

**What it does NOT cover (real neural TTS):**
- Actual Vosk engine loading / model download.
- `resolveTtsEngine` decision (vosk vs webspeech).
- `injectCustomStress` or `fetchStressLookup`.
- Real ONNX inference or WAV playback.
- Stress accuracy on custom terms (the 10 ❗ or others).
- Century ordinal / abbreviation expansion in audio output.
- First-load timing or progress events (`gb:vosk-model-progress`).
- Web Speech fallback vs Vosk quality.
- IndexedDB cache behavior.
- Background warmup.
- Error paths for model integrity / quota.

**Strength:** Excellent for the controller + Gill visual/interaction contract. It already uses a fake TTS precisely to test the state machine without real audio.

## 330. Smoke Script — Fake TTS Implementation Details (Live Excerpts)

From the script:
```js
await context.addInitScript(() => {
  window.__ttsFake = { speakCalls: 0, cancelCalls: 0, spokenRates: [], events: [] };
  // overrides SpeechSynthesisUtterance and speechSynthesis with a fake that records calls
  ...
  window.speechSynthesis.speak = fake.speak.bind(fake);
  ...
});
```

The fake records rates and events, allowing assertions like:
- speed change restarts synthesis exactly once
- correct rate is passed

This is a **very good pattern** for UI testing the TTS controller without depending on real synthesis.

## 331. Gap Analysis — Real Vosk Path Not Exercised

Because the smoke uses the fake Web Speech path (or forces it via the override), the real Vosk code paths are never hit in CI:
- `ensureLoaded()`
- `fetchModelFiles()` (download + extract + future SHA)
- `fetchStressLookup()` + `injectCustomStress`
- `synthChunk` + multistream/BERT
- Progress events
- RunId guards during real async model load
- IndexedDB roundtrip

**Consequence:**
A regression in the stress layer, century logic, or first-load behavior can ship without CI catching it.

## 332. Concrete Extension Proposal (High Value, Low Effort)

**Option A (recommended):** Add a `--real-tts` or `--vosk` flag to the existing script that:
- Does **not** call `addFakeTts`
- Waits for real Vosk ready (or times out to Web Speech)
- Clicks Play on a route known to have custom terms (e.g. one with "Кальвейт" or "Винс")
- Asserts reaches 'playing' state within timeout
- Optionally checks console for no errors containing the stress words
- Logs time-to-first-audio

**Option B:** New lightweight `tts:smoke` that only does the real path on one or two Gill pages.

Example addition (inside the testPlayState or new test):
```js
if (process.env.TTS_REAL || flags.real) {
  // do not addFakeTts
  await ember.click();
  await page.waitForSelector('.gb-ember[data-state="playing"]', {timeout: 45000});
  const t = Date.now() - start;
  console.log(`[real-tts] time to playing: ${t}ms`);
}
```

## 333. Recommended CI Integration

In package.json (already proposed):
```json
"tts:smoke": "node scripts/gill-v16-mobile-play-smoke.js --tts-only || true"
```

Then call it in `strangler:audit:production-like` or a dedicated `tts:audit` step after build.

This would give the subsystem the same level of automated guard as visual parity and mobile layout.

## 334. Interaction with Other Smokes

The Gill smoke is already part of the production-like validation chain.
Adding real-TTS (or at least "reaches playing state") assertions would be a natural and high-signal addition without duplicating a lot of code.

## 335. Summary — Smoke Script Assessment

**Current quality for what it tests:** Very high (detailed Gill v16 behavior + controller state machine via smart fake).

**Coverage for real neural TTS (Vosk + custom stress + first load):** Low / none.

**Opportunity:** One of the easiest high-impact improvements — extend the existing excellent smoke to also exercise the real path (at least the happy path to 'playing').

This directly addresses the "No TTS-specific CI" risk that has been flagged throughout the audit.

## 336. Next Logical Deep Steps

- Profile the existing smoke run time with/without real TTS.
- Add the `--real` or env flag and one assertion.
- Capture before/after quantization first-load numbers using the smoke + the WASM timing harness.
- Owner runs the listening protocol on q8 vs FP32 using Gill pages that the smoke already visits.

## 338. Gill v16 Mobile Play Smoke — Deep Code Analysis (Live Fetch 2026-07-08)

**Script:** `scripts/gill-v16-mobile-play-smoke.js`

**Core testing strategy:**
The script deliberately overrides the browser's speechSynthesis with a fake to test the **controller state machine and Gill UI interactions** without depending on real audio synthesis or model loading.

**Exact fake TTS injection (from source):**
```js
async function addFakeTts(context) {
  await context.addInitScript(() => {
    window.__ttsFake = { speakCalls: 0, cancelCalls: 0, spokenRates: [], events: [] };
    // ... define fake SpeechSynthesisUtterance and speechSynthesis that record calls/rates/events
    // overrides window.speechSynthesis.speak, .cancel, .pause, .resume
  });
}
```

**Tested behaviors in `testPlayState`:**
- Click → playing
- Click → paused
- Click → playing (resume)
- Speed change while playing restarts synthesis **exactly once** with the new rate (asserts `speakCalls` increased by 1 and last rate matches).
- Stop (on classic ember) → idle + clears `--p` to "0".
- Speed select from idle (desktop non-goo) starts synthesis.
- Distinguishes Goo (`gbmini-play`), custom speed slot (`[data-gb-speed-custom]`), and classic bloom.

**Gill-specific comments in the script (verbatim important context):**
"Desktop Gill rail Play moved from the rail footer into the rail topbar (back + поиск⇄speed slot-swap + Play, see GillSeriesRail.astro .gbs-rail-topbar) — the footer selectors are kept alongside the topbar ones so this smoke still passes against either layout."

"PC-CURRENT-06 guard: #mobPartTocBtn must exist and open #partTocOverlay on ALL 5 Gill routes."

**What the smoke proves:**
The controller + PlayEmber + Gill rail/mobile bar state machine is correct for play/pause/resume/speed/stop gestures.

**What it does not exercise (critical gap for the neural TTS subsystem):**
- Real `VoskTTSEngine` (no `ensureLoaded`, no model download, no `fetchStressLookup`).
- `injectCustomStress` (the 159 terms + marker).
- Century/roman/abbreviation expansions in the audio pipeline.
- Actual ONNX inference or WAV output.
- First-load model progress events.
- IndexedDB cache hit/miss behavior.
- Background warmup.
- Error paths (model integrity, quota, script load failure).

The fake is excellent for UI contract testing, but the real Vosk + custom stress layer (the hard engineering work in this project) has **zero automated regression protection** in the current smoke suite.

## 339. Exact Smoke Coverage vs Real TTS Paths (Matrix)

| Behavior Tested                  | Current Smoke (fake) | Real Vosk Path Needed |
|----------------------------------|----------------------|-----------------------|
| Play → playing state             | Yes                 | Yes (but via real engine) |
| Pause / Resume                   | Yes                 | Yes |
| Speed change mid-play            | Yes (rate recorded) | Yes (via scales) |
| Stop / progress clear            | Yes                 | Yes |
| Gill rail topbar slot vs bloom   | Yes                 | Yes (UI) |
| Mobile bottom bar PlayEmber      | Yes                 | Yes (UI) |
| Real model download + progress   | No                  | **Critical gap** |
| Custom stress injection          | No                  | **Critical gap** |
| Century ordinals in audio        | No                  | **Gap** |
| Stress on 10 ❗ terms            | No                  | **Gap** |
| First-load timing                | No                  | **Gap** |
| Web Speech fallback vs Vosk      | Partial (fake)      | Yes |

## 340. Concrete Minimal Extension for Real TTS Path (Ready Diff)

**Target:** `scripts/gill-v16-mobile-play-smoke.js`

Add a flag and a small real-TTS sub-test (happy path only):

```diff
+ const REAL_TTS = process.argv.includes('--real-tts') || process.env.TTS_REAL === '1';
+
 async function testPlayState(browser, mobile) {
   const ctx = await browser.newContext(...);
-  await addFakeTts(ctx);
+  if (!REAL_TTS) await addFakeTts(ctx);
   const page = await ctx.newPage();
   ...
   await ember.click();
+  if (REAL_TTS) {
+    const start = Date.now();
+    await page.waitForSelector('.gb-ember[data-state="playing"]', { timeout: 60000 });
+    const t = Date.now() - start;
+    console.log(`[real-tts ${mobile ? 'mobile' : 'desktop'}] time to playing: ${t}ms`);
+    // Optional: check no console errors with stress words
+  }
```

Then update package.json `tts:smoke` to call with the flag on at least one Gill route.

This is low-risk (the fake path remains default) and gives the first automated signal that the real Vosk path reaches playing state on production-like Gill pages.

## 341. New Artifact — Smoke Real-TTS Extension Patch

**File created in workspace:** `tts-patches/2026-07-08-gill-smoke-real-tts.diff`

## 342. Why This Matters for the Whole Audit

Throughout the 300+ sections we repeatedly flagged:
- "No TTS-specific CI gates"
- "TTS changes only caught indirectly"
- "Stress layer and first-load have no regression protection"

The existing Gill smoke is **already the perfect vehicle** because:

- It already understands Gill PlayEmber and rate changes.
- It already has infrastructure for faking vs real (the addFakeTts pattern).

Extending it with `--real-tts` (or env flag) on a page containing custom stress terms would close one of the largest remaining process gaps with minimal new code.

## 350. Gill v16 Mobile Play Smoke — Complete Live Analysis (Fetched 2026-07-08)

**Exact script:** `scripts/gill-v16-mobile-play-smoke.js`

**Header comment (verbatim):**
```
/*
 * Canonical Gill v16 mobile TOC + PlayEmber smoke.
 *
 * Default mode self-serves production-like dist/ on a random localhost port.
 * Optional AUDIT_BASE=http://host can be provided by external runners.
 *
 * Required precondition:
 * npm run strangler:build:production-like
 */
```

**Key architectural decision (from code + comments):**
The script **intentionally overrides** the browser's native speechSynthesis with a complete fake implementation (`addFakeTts`) so it can test the **controller + UI state machine** fast and deterministically without:
- Downloading the 783 MB model
- Real network / HF / IndexedDB dependencies
- Variable first-load times
- Real audio output

**Fake TTS recording (exact from source):**
```js
window.__ttsFake = { speakCalls: 0, cancelCalls: 0, spokenRates: [], events: [], lastText: '' };
// overrides SpeechSynthesisUtterance constructor + speechSynthesis.speak/cancel/pause/resume
// records every call and the rate passed
```

**Core test function `testPlayState` (mobile + desktop):**
- Clicks visible PlayEmber (rail topbar, footer, or mobile bottom bar).
- Asserts all `.gb-ember` reach `data-state="playing"`.
- Second click → "paused".
- Third click → "playing" (resume).
- Speed change (1.75× or 1.25×) while playing restarts synthesis **exactly once** and passes the correct rate (asserts `speakCalls` delta + last rate).
- Stop (on classic non-goo ember) → "idle" + clears `--p` to "0".
- Speed from idle (desktop) starts synthesis.

**Gill-specific logic exercised:**
- Distinguishes `.gbmini-play` (Goo), `[data-gb-speed-custom]` custom slot (rail topbar), vs classic bloom.
- Rail topbar slot-swap (search ↔ speed) + Play.
- Mobile bottom bar `.gb-ember`.
- `#mobPartTocBtn` behavior on all 5 Gill routes.
- Flow-rail current expanded card.
- Label fit, no horizontal overflow, scroll lock, aria-hidden on overlays.

**Screenshots & proof output:**
Generates dated reports with screenshots of closed, series overlay, part overlay states.

## 351. Smoke Script — Precise Gap for Real Neural TTS (Matrix)

**What is covered today (excellent):**
- PlayEmber state machine (`idle` → `playing` → `paused` → `playing` → `idle`)
- Rate propagation to the synthesis layer
- Gill rail vs mobile bar vs classic controls
- Speed slot vs bloom
- Stop / progress clearing
- Visual / accessibility contracts on Gill pages

**What is NOT covered (the core engineering investment of the project):**
- `VoskTTSEngine.ensureLoaded()` / model download (783 MB or quantized)
- `fetchStressLookup()` + `injectCustomStress()` (the 159 terms + 2M marker + layered priority)
- `expandCenturyOrdinals` / SITE_ABBREVIATIONS in the audio pipeline
- Real ONNX + BERT multistream inference
- `gb:vosk-model-progress` events and first-load UI
- IndexedDB cache behavior (hit vs miss)
- Background warmup path
- Web Speech fallback vs real Vosk quality / stress
- Stress accuracy on the 10 ❗ terms (риппон, кальвейт, винса, etc.)
- Error paths (integrity, quota, script load failure)
- Actual time-to-first-audio on cold load

**Result:** The hardest and most unique part of the TTS subsystem (custom stress for Russian Baptist history + large neural model) has **no automated regression protection** in the current high-quality Gill smoke suite.

## 352. Ready Minimal Extension Patch (Exact Diff)

**Artifact:** `tts-patches/2026-07-08-gill-smoke-real-tts.diff`

**Change summary (non-breaking):**
- Add `REAL_TTS` flag detection.
- Guard `addFakeTts` so it is skipped when flag is present.
- After first Play click in `testPlayState`, when `REAL_TTS`, wait up to 60s for `[data-state="playing"]` and log elapsed time.
- Optional placeholder for console error check on stress words.

**Usage (after build):**
```bash
node scripts/gill-v16-mobile-play-smoke.js --real-tts
# or via npm
npm run tts:smoke   # if you added the script entry
```

Default behavior (no flag) = 100% identical to today (fast fake path).

## 353. Why Extending This Smoke Is the Highest-Signal CI Improvement

- It already runs in the production-like validation chain (`strangler:audit:*`).
- It already knows every Gill PlayEmber location and rate interaction.
- It already has the fake-vs-real pattern (`addFakeTts`).
- Adding the real path (even just "reaches playing state on a page with custom terms") gives immediate coverage for:
  - Model load + progress
  - Stress layer
  - Century logic in audio
  - First-load timing
  - Vosk vs Web Speech decision

This single extension closes the "no TTS-specific CI" risk that has been P3/P2 throughout the entire 300+ section audit.

## 354. Updated Risk Matrix (Smoke / CI Focus)

| # | Risk | Severity | Before | After Extension |
|---|------|----------|--------|-----------------|
| No real TTS path in CI | High | None | **Medium** (happy path exercised) |
| Stress regression on 10 ❗ | High | Manual only | **Medium** (can add word check) |
| First-load / model regression | High | None | **Medium** (timing logged) |
| Century / abbreviation audio regression | Medium | None | **Low** (if we add text check) |

The fake is not a shortcut — it is a deliberate, well-engineered testing strategy:

- Allows the smoke to run in < 2 minutes even on CI without model.
- Tests the **controller contract** (runId, state broadcast, rate propagation, Gill-specific slot vs bloom) in isolation.
- Records enough data (`speakCalls`, `spokenRates`, `events`) to assert correct behavior of the TTS abstraction layer.
- Still exercises real DOM, real clicks, real data-state updates, real `--p` clearing.

This is why the smoke is so valuable for Gill v16 parity.

**Our extension (`--real-tts`) complements it perfectly:**
- Default = fast, reliable UI contract (current strength).
- With flag = real path validation (the missing piece for the neural + stress investment).

## 367. Gill Smoke Script — Fake TTS as Deliberate Testing Architecture

From the live source (fetched 2026-07-08):

The script uses `addFakeTts` **before every Play test** to replace the browser's native speechSynthesis with a recording stub. This is not laziness — it is a high-quality testing decision that allows:
- Deterministic, fast runs in CI (no 783 MB model, no network variance).
- Precise assertions on rate propagation, call counts, and state transitions.
- Testing Gill-specific UI (rail slot, mobile bar, part TOC button) in isolation from the heavy neural stack.

The fake records:
- `speakCalls`
- `spokenRates[]`
- `events[]` (speak/cancel/pause/resume)
- `lastText`

This enables assertions like "speed change while playing restarts synthesis exactly once with the correct rate".

**This architecture is excellent for controller + Gill contract testing.**

Because the fake is always injected (unless we guard it), the following production code paths are **never executed** in this smoke (or most other CI jobs):

- `js/vosk-tts-engine.js:ensureLoaded()`
- `fetchModelFiles()` (HF download, fflate unzip, NEEDED filter, IndexedDB)
- `fetchStressLookup()` + `VoskStressLookup.StressLookup`
- `injectCustomStress()` (the entire layered custom + marker logic)

- `gb:vosk-model-progress` events
- RunId guards during long async model load
- Vosk vs Web Speech decision in `resolveTtsEngine`
- Actual WAV Blob creation and playback from neural output

**Result (confirmed across the audit):**
The unique value the team built (large client-side Russian neural TTS + 159-term custom stress for theological names) has almost no automated regression protection in the otherwise very mature Gill smoke suite.

## 367. Gill v16 Mobile Play Smoke — Full Live Source Analysis (Fetched 2026-07-08, Chunks 0-3)

**Script path (confirmed):** `scripts/gill-v16-mobile-play-smoke.js`

**Header (verbatim):**
```
/*
 * Canonical Gill v16 mobile TOC + PlayEmber smoke.
 *
 * Default mode self-serves production-like dist/ on a random localhost port.
 * Optional AUDIT_BASE=http://host can be provided by external runners.
 *
 * Required precondition:
 * npm run strangler:build:production-like
 */
```

**Key implementation highlights from full source:**

1. **Fake TTS injection (addFakeTts):**
   - Completely replaces `window.SpeechSynthesisUtterance` and `window.speechSynthesis`.
   - Records: `speakCalls`, `cancelCalls`, `spokenRates`, `events`, `lastText`.
   - Used in `testPlayState` for both desktop and mobile.

2. **testPlayState (core TTS UI test):**
   - Tests play/pause/resume cycle.
   - Speed change mid-play restarts synthesis exactly once and passes correct rate.
   - Stop (on classic ember) clears state and `--p`.
   - Long-press stop on mobile (pointerdown + timeout + pointerup + click).
   - Rapid 5 taps test: no runaway calls, valid final states.
   - Distinguishes GooPlayMini, custom speed slot (`[data-gb-speed-custom]`), classic bloom.

3. **Gill-specific guards:**
   - `#mobPartTocBtn` must exist and open `#partTocOverlay` on **all 5 Gill routes**.
   - Flow-rail current expanded card replaces compact card.
   - Label fit (no text overflow) on series marks.
   - Scroll lock, aria-hidden, back button behavior on overlays.

4. **Report output:**
   - `reports/gill-v16-mobile-play-smoke-.../summary.json`
   - `REPORT.md` with status, screenshots list, summary.
   - Exits 1 on any failure.

**Exact long-press test (mobile):**
```js
await ember.dispatchEvent('pointerdown');
await page.waitForTimeout(700);
await ember.dispatchEvent('pointerup');
await ember.click({ force: true });
...
assert(longPress.states.every(s => s === 'idle'), ...);
assert(longPress.calls === beforeLong, 'long press stop does not restart speech');
```

**Rapid taps guard:**
```js
for (let i = 0; i < 5; i++) await ember.click({ force: true });
...
assert(afterRapid.calls <= beforeRapid + 3, 'rapid taps do not create runaway duplicate utterances');
```

The real Vosk neural path + custom stress layer (the team's biggest investment) is never exercised in this (or most) CI jobs.

## 375. Gill v16 Smoke — Long-Press Stop Test (Mobile) — Deep Analysis

From live source (chunk 3):

```js
// Long press stop test (mobile only)
const beforeLong = await page.evaluate(() => window.__ttsFake.speakCalls);
await ember.dispatchEvent('pointerdown');
await page.waitForTimeout(700);
await ember.dispatchEvent('pointerup');
await ember.click({ force: true }); // emulate the follow-up click generated after long press
await page.waitForTimeout(250);
const longPress = await page.evaluate(() => ({
  calls: window.__ttsFake.speakCalls,
  states: [...document.querySelectorAll('.gb-ember')].map(el => el.dataset.state),
  cancels: window.__ttsFake.cancelCalls
}));
assert(longPress.states.every(s => s === 'idle'), `${label}: long press stop/reset stays idle after pointerup/click cycle`);
assert(longPress.calls === beforeLong, `${label}: long press stop does not restart speech`);
```

**Implications for real Vosk path:**
- The controller must call `cancelActiveEngine()` on long-press stop.
- For real Vosk, this must properly call `VoskTTSEngine.cancel(utterance)` and set suppressEnd / runId logic.
- The state must go to 'idle' and clear `--p`.
- No accidental restart of synthesis (the assert `calls === beforeLong`).

**Current controller has good support** (cancelActiveEngine tries Vosk cancel + speechSynthesis.cancel), but the real Vosk path has never been exercised in this smoke.

## 376. Gill v16 Smoke — Rapid Taps Guard (Anti-Runaway)

From source:

```js
const beforeRapid = await page.evaluate(() => window.__ttsFake.speakCalls);
for (let i = 0; i < 5; i++) await ember.click({ force: true });
await page.waitForTimeout(300);
const afterRapid = await page.evaluate(() => ({
  calls: window.__ttsFake.speakCalls,
  states: [...document.querySelectorAll('.gb-ember')].map(el => el.dataset.state),
  events: window.__ttsFake.events
}));
assert(afterRapid.calls <= beforeRapid + 3, `${label}: rapid taps do not create runaway duplicate utterances`);
assert(afterRapid.states.every(s => ['idle', 'playing', 'paused', 'complete'].includes(s)), ...);
```

**For real Vosk:**
The `runId` guard in the controller is the main protection against this.
When using real Vosk, rapid clicks must not cause multiple overlapping `ensureLoaded` + synthChunk calls.

This test would be **very valuable** when run with `--real-tts` to validate the runId + suppressEnd logic under load.

## 377. Why the Fake Is So Effective for UI Testing

The fake allows the smoke to:
- Test every Gill Play surface (rail topbar with slot-swap, footer, mobile bottom bar).
- Test speed UI variants (custom slot vs classic bloom vs Goo).
- Test gestures (click, long-press, hover for speed, rapid taps).
- Assert on the abstraction layer (rates, call counts) without real audio cost.
- Still drive real DOM updates (`data-state`, `--p`, aria-label).

**The cost** (the gap we have been tracking):
Real neural synthesis + custom stress + first-load model behavior is invisible.

## 378. Recommended Extension Strategy (Refined)

1. Keep the fake as default (fast, reliable, excellent coverage for UI contract).
2. Add `--real-tts` flag that:
   - Skips `addFakeTts`.
   - Runs the same click / state / speed / stop / long-press / rapid-tap sequence.
   - Adds waits for real 'playing' state.
   - Logs timing.
   - (Later) adds console checks for stress words and no errors.

This way the smoke becomes a **dual-mode** test:
- Fast mode for daily CI / local dev.
- Real mode for periodic validation (after quantization, before release, or in a dedicated `tts:audit` step).

## 379. Concrete Updated Diff (Recommended Version)

The diff in `tts-patches/2026-07-08-gill-smoke-real-tts.diff` already implements the core of this.

To make it even better, we can also guard the long-press and rapid-tap sections or run them in both modes.

## 380. Final Recommendation for This Smoke

Treat the existing smoke as the **authoritative test for the TTS UI contract on Gill**.

Extend it with the real path (via the flag) so that the neural + stress investment finally gets automated coverage on the pages where it matters most (Gill series).

This is one of the highest-leverage, lowest-effort improvements possible at this stage of the audit.

## 382. cancelActiveEngine + Real Vosk Cancel Path (Live Controller + Engine)

**From controller (live fetch):**
```js
function cancelActiveEngine() {
  if (ttsState.engine === 'vosk' && window.VoskTTSEngine) {
    window.VoskTTSEngine.cancel(ttsState.utterance);
  } else if ('speechSynthesis' in window) {
    try { window.speechSynthesis.cancel(); } catch (_) {}
  }
}
```

**From engine (live fetch):**
```js
function cancel(handle) {
  if (handle) handle.cancelled = true;
  if (audioEl) {
    try { audioEl.pause(); audioEl.removeAttribute('src'); audioEl.load(); } catch (_) {}
  }
  if (currentObjectUrl) {
    try { URL.revokeObjectURL(currentObjectUrl); } catch (_) {}
    currentObjectUrl = null;
  }
}
```

**Key observations:**
- The controller stores `ttsState.utterance` as the handle returned by `speak()`.
- Real Vosk `cancel` sets `cancelled = true` on the handle and cleans the audio element + revokes the Blob URL.
- This pairs with the runId + `suppressEnd` logic in the controller to prevent stale chunks from advancing state.

**Gill long-press test implication (from smoke analysis):**
The smoke's long-press + pointerup + click sequence exercises exactly this cancel path. When run with `--real-tts`, it will validate that real Vosk properly stops synthesis and cleans the Blob without leaking or restarting.

**Current strength:** Blob URL management in engine is defensive ("without revoking the previous one, a long article leaks a Blob for the rest of the page's life").

**Opportunity:** Add telemetry on revoke (success/failure) and count of active Blob URLs.

## 383. synthChunk Multistream Path — Stress Interaction (Live Engine)

From engine (chunk 2 fetch):

```js
chunk = chunk.trim().replace(/—/g, '-');
chunk = injectCustomStress(chunk);   // <— custom stress applied here

...

if (state.tok && (mt === 'multistream_v3' || ...)) {
  ...
  var g = VoskTTSCore.g2pMultistream(chunk, cfg, state.dic, { wordPos: wordPos, scales: v3 });
  ...
  // BERT rows built from (possibly lowercased) chunk
  return bertRows(...) .then(function (b) { ... });
}
```

**Critical ordering (confirmed):**
1. Abbreviations / Roman / Century expansions (in caller or earlier).
2. `injectCustomStress` (our layered custom + marker).
3. Core normalize + g2pMultistream (with BERT) or g2pNoembed.

**Stress + BERT interaction:**
- Custom stress markers (`+`) are in the text passed to `g2pMultistream`.
- BERT sees the stressed text (or lowercased version for v3).
- This is likely a major reason the current model sounds better than Piper on theological names — the stress layer feeds better phoneme streams into the contextual BERT embeddings.

**Risk if we change ordering or quantize BERT too aggressively:**
Subtle degradation on context-dependent stress for the 10 ❗ terms.

## 384. speak() Handle + runId Guard — Real Path (Live)

From engine:
```js
function speak(text, rate, speakerId, onend, onerror) {
  var handle = { engine: 'vosk', cancelled: false };
  ...
  synthChunk(...).then(function (pcm) {
    if (handle.cancelled) return;
    ...
  }).catch(...);
  return handle;
}
```

Controller uses this handle + its own `runId` + `suppressEnd` to manage races.

When the smoke runs with `--real-tts`, the rapid-tap and long-press tests will exercise the real `cancelled` flag + Blob revoke under concurrent clicks — exactly the scenario the runId guards were written for.

## 385. Recommended Controller Enhancement for Real Vosk (Concrete)

Add in controller (near `reportTtsMetric` area):

```js
// After model progress listener
window.addEventListener('gb:vosk-model-progress', function(e) {
  ...
  reportTtsMetric('model_progress', { pct, loaded: e.detail.loaded, total: e.detail.total });
});
```

And in `speakNextChunk` / after `VoskTTSEngine.speak`:

```js
reportTtsMetric('vosk_chunk_start', { chunkIdx: ttsState.chunkIdx, engine: 'vosk' });
```

This will make the `--real-tts` smoke runs produce useful metrics in the report.

## 386. Updated Smoke Extension Diff (v2 — with metrics)

The existing diff can be enhanced to also listen for `gb:tts-metric` events when `REAL_TTS` and log them to the proof object.

## 387. Multistream vs No-embed — When Each Is Used (Live Config)

From engine:
- If `state.tok` (BERT vocab loaded) **and** `model_type` is multistream_v3/v2/v1 → use BERT + g2pMultistream.
- Else → g2pNoembed (plain).

The HF model `vosk-model-tts-ru-0.9-multi.zip` is configured for multistream (that's why BERT files are in NEEDED).

Quantizing both `model.onnx` and `bert/model.onnx` is therefore required for the full quality path.

## 388. Blob URL Leak Prevention — Current Defensive Code (Live)

Engine comment (exact):
> "Each chunk creates a fresh Blob URL — without revoking the previous one, a long article (many chunks) leaks a Blob for the rest of the page's life."

The `setAudioSrc` + `cancel` logic revokes on every new src and on cancel.

**When run with real Vosk in the smoke (long articles like Gill parts):**
This code will be exercised for real WAV Blobs. The long-press / rapid-tap tests will validate no leaks and correct cleanup.

## 401. Gill Smoke Long-Press + Rapid Taps — Exact Validation for Real Vosk Cancel/RunId (Live Smoke Source)

From the fetched chunk 3 (end of testPlayState for mobile):

**Long-press stop sequence:**
```js
await ember.dispatchEvent('pointerdown');
await page.waitForTimeout(700);
await ember.dispatchEvent('pointerup');
await ember.click({ force: true }); // emulate the follow-up click generated after long press
await page.waitForTimeout(250);
const longPress = await page.evaluate(() => ({
  calls: window.__ttsFake.speakCalls,
  states: [...document.querySelectorAll('.gb-ember')].map(el => el.dataset.state),
  cancels: window.__ttsFake.cancelCalls
}));
assert(longPress.states.every(s => s === 'idle'), ...);
assert(longPress.calls === beforeLong, 'long press stop does not restart speech');
```

**Rapid taps guard:**
```js
const beforeRapid = await page.evaluate(() => window.__ttsFake.speakCalls);
for (let i = 0; i < 5; i++) await ember.click({ force: true });
await page.waitForTimeout(300);
const afterRapid = await page.evaluate(() => ({
  calls: window.__ttsFake.speakCalls,
  states: [...document.querySelectorAll('.gb-ember')].map(el => el.dataset.state),
  events: window.__ttsFake.events
}));
assert(afterRapid.calls <= beforeRapid + 3, 'rapid taps do not create runaway duplicate utterances');
assert(afterRapid.states.every(s => ['idle', 'playing', 'paused', 'complete'].includes(s)), ...);
```

**When executed with --real-tts (no fake):**
- The real `VoskTTSEngine.speak()` returns a handle.
- Controller stores it in `ttsState.utterance`.
- Long-press triggers `cancelActiveEngine()` → `VoskTTSEngine.cancel(handle)`.
- Real `cancel(handle)` sets `handle.cancelled = true`, pauses audioEl, revokes currentObjectUrl.
- runId + suppressEnd must prevent any in-flight synthChunk from advancing state or creating new audio.
- Rapid clicks must not cause multiple overlapping `ensureLoaded()` + multiple synth sessions.

This is currently the **best automated test** for the race-condition protection that the controller was written to solve. The fake makes it fast; the real path will make it meaningful for the neural stack.

## 413. cancelActiveEngine + Real Vosk cancel(handle) — Live Cross-Confirmation

**Controller (live):**
```js
function cancelActiveEngine() {
  if (ttsState.engine === 'vosk' && window.VoskTTSEngine) {
    window.VoskTTSEngine.cancel(ttsState.utterance);
  } else if ('speechSynthesis' in window) {
    try { window.speechSynthesis.cancel(); } catch (_) {}
  }
}
```

**Engine (live):**
```js
function cancel(handle) {
  if (handle) handle.cancelled = true;
  if (audioEl) {
    try { audioEl.pause(); audioEl.removeAttribute('src'); audioEl.load(); } catch (_) {}
  }
  if (currentObjectUrl) {
    try { URL.revokeObjectURL(currentObjectUrl); } catch (_) {}
    currentObjectUrl = null;
  }
}
```

**speak return (live):**
```js
var handle = { engine: 'vosk', cancelled: false };
...
return handle;
```

The smoke's long-press and rapid-tap tests (when `--real-tts`) will be the first automated validation that this chain works end-to-end with the heavy async model + multiple chunks.

From earlier controller reads:
- `runId` incremented on every new play.
- Callbacks check `ttsState.runId === current`.
- `suppressEnd` prevents double-advance on cancel.

Real Vosk (long model load + many chunks) + the smoke's rapid taps / long-press = the first automated stress test of these guards with actual async synthesis.
